    "role": "user",
    "content": "Below is the full consultation dialogue so far. Reply naturally in English as the Patient to the last Doctor utterance.\nAnswer only the question, in 2–3 sentences maximum.\n\n[Dialogue]\nDoctor: Hello, I’m glad you came in today. What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Hi doctor. I've been having a really hard time staying in my seat lately, whether I'm at work in meetings or out at dinner. I just feel this constant urge to get up and move around, and it's starting to cause some real issues for me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period lasting several days when you felt unusually energetic or euphoric, needed much less sleep, and were more impulsive or talkative than usual? Or has this restlessness been more constant over time?\n\nPatient: No, I've never had those short phases of feeling euphoric or needing less sleep. This restlessness and my tendency to talk too much have been constant for about a year and two months now, and honestly, these kinds of struggles go all the way back to when I was a kid before age 12.\n\nDoctor: Along with the restlessness and talking a lot, do you also often have trouble paying attention, staying organized, or finishing tasks? For example, do you get easily distracted or lose things often?\n\nPatient: I definitely struggle with paying attention, mostly because I tend to make a lot of careless mistakes and miss important details. I'm not really sure about losing things or having trouble with organization, as those aren't really issues for me. It's really just the careless errors and lack of attention to detail that I notice happening a lot.\n\nDoctor: Besides careless mistakes, do you also often have trouble staying focused on tasks or conversations, avoiding tasks that need sustained mental effort, or not seeming to listen when spoken to directly?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really experience those specific difficulties with staying focused, avoiding tasks, or not listening. Like I mentioned, my main issue in that area is just making careless mistakes and missing details.\n\nDoctor: Besides difficulty staying seated and talking a lot, do you often feel driven like a motor, blurt out answers, interrupt others, or have trouble waiting your turn?\n\nPatient: No, I don't really experience those things—I don't feel driven by a motor, blurt out answers, or have trouble waiting my turn. For me, the real challenges are definitely just my struggle to stay in my seat and my tendency to talk excessively.\n\nDoctor: Do these symptoms cause problems in more than one setting, like both work and home or social situations, and have they been present most of the time for at least 6 months?\n\nPatient: Yes, these symptoms have been going on constantly for about a year and two months now, so well over six months. They definitely cause a lot of trouble in multiple places, both at work and at home, especially with my tendency to make careless mistakes, talk excessively, and struggle to stay in my seat.\n\nDoctor: Before age 12, did you also have several other attention problems besides careless mistakes, like being easily distracted, losing things, forgetting tasks, or not finishing schoolwork?\n\nPatient: I don't really recall having those specific problems like losing things, forgetting tasks, or being easily distracted before age 12. Back then, it was really just the careless mistakes and struggling to sit still.\n\nDoctor: Before age 12, besides struggling to sit still, did you also often talk excessively, fidget, run about when it wasn’t appropriate, or have trouble doing quiet activities calmly?"
  }

Subtypes: Impulsive Anger, Verbal Aggression, Physical Aggression, Chronic Irritability\n[S078] Reckless_Self_Destructive_Behavior: Reckless or self-destructive behavior pattern that endangers one's safety or health.
